ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-02-sejm-31-4.u339	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I am pleased to present a draft law on the regulation of property rights in the capital city of Warsaw. I am very sorry that we are not debating this project until today. We want to defend those who were taken away in 1945 and their property in the years to come. The adoption of this law will end this treatment of tenants, end the activity of house cleaners. We want to finally, comprehensively and comprehensively regulate the rights to Warsaw real estate in the area of m.st. Warsaw received under the Warsaw Decree. The purpose of the Act is therefore to make amends for infringement of property rights, respecting the principles of historical and social justice and the constitutional principle of protection of property rights. But the purpose of this law is also to solve the great social problem and the great development problem for our capital. The decree of Bierut was, in fact, a bad law, unjust, while the practice of its application both during communism and now unfortunately brings a pitiful result and it is high time to change that. If you allow me, I will start with what Bierut's decree was, because much is said about what reprivatization should look like, comparing Warsaw reprivatization to reprivatization throughout the country, forgetting that Bieruta's decree was a law strictly attached to the city, was a decree in force in one particular city, in the capital of Poland, even though it was issued from the central level. The decree of Bierut, also known as the Warsaw Decree, is a decree issued on 26 October 1945 by the National Council of which Bolesław Bierut was president. This is an important statement and I would like to stress this strongly: it was a decree issued from the national level, it was not a decree issued from the local government level or from the Warsaw level. By its power, it owns the commune of M.S. Warsaw passed all land within the pre-war borders of the city. Warsaw. The decree wasn't about buildings, it was about land. Buildings located on nationalised land were to remain the property of the rightful owners if they applied for a decree, so it was probably the first law in Poland's history to solve or separate property ownership from ownership of the property. The decree concerned around 14,000. ha and about 28,000. buildings. Despite its nationalist nature, the decree provided for the possibility of restitution of property in the form of property rights of buildings and in the future of perpetual lease rights. Following an appropriate decree request, the former owner should be able to recover the claim on this ground. According to the law, the municipality was obliged to take such requests into account, unfortunately the practice of applying this decree led to the fact that these applications were largely eliminated, that there were mass refusals or that decisions were not issued in this regard at all. In 1989, when Poland regained some sovereignty, many rightful owners returned to the proposals it had submitted several decades earlier. On this basis, most refunds are made in Warsaw under the decree of Bierut. I wanted to emphasize very strongly, because I will talk about this when it comes to the concrete solutions set out in this law, that the deadline for submitting the so-called. a decree proposal, i.e. a proposal which was to grant the right to apply, the right to the building, was a binding 12-month deadline. In many cases, it has been impossible for legitimate owners to collect relevant documents within that period. Let us remember, however, that we are talking about a situation where the whole city was destroyed, where access to the books was difficult, where documents confirming property could be destroyed and it was very difficult to recreate them. But there were also situations where many of these rightful owners simply couldn't apply because either they had no contact with their own families, or many of them just sat in prison. It is worth mentioning this now, because it was the real effect of the decree of Bierut, which we also have to face in this bill. We have solutions for that. I'll talk about it in a minute. Ladies and gentlemen! The law we propose sets out the rules for granting and implementing the right to establish the use of perpetual real estate robbed by the decree of Bierut, i.e. de facto what should have been realized several decades ago. Now, where it is possible, it may be realized. I'm talking about those people who were deprived of their property, and they couldn't file that motion. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-02-sejm-31-4.u394	Thank you. Mr Tomasz Romankowski, Kukiz Poselski Club15, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-02-sejm-31-4.u395	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Poselski Club15 on the government draft law on specific rules for the removal of legal effects of reprivatization decisions concerning Warsaw real estate issued in violation of the law. This draft law regulates the special procedure for dealing with the removal of legal effects of re-privatization decisions concerning Warsaw real estate issued in violation of the law, the issues of the authorities competent for this proceeding and the effects of civil law decisions issued in that procedure. It provides for the setting up of a committee on the removal of the legal effects of reprivatisation decisions. It is also intended to be the task of the committee to address the competent authorities in the course of proceedings before the committee of the existence of circumstances conducive to the adoption of decisions in breach of the law or the execution of a crime or obstructing its disclosure. In front of the committee, there is a social advisory and advisory board. According to the provisions of the proposed law, the chairman of the committee may request the prosecution to search premises or other places or to seize things in order to secure evidence on or to secure the return of the equivalent of the undue benefit. According to the proposed provisions, the committee carries out an open hearing in the proceedings, but transparency may be excluded for example. for the security of the state, the threat of peace and public order, and therefore practically always, because the concept of a threat to peace, public order is a very broad concept and indiscretion. Meetings outside the committee shall be classified. The committee shall be responsible for the summons for the hearing of the parties, witnesses and experts. A fine from PLN 1000 to PLN 3000 shall be imposed on persons who do not appear or have left the trial without the permission of the hearing manager. It is up to the committee, by way of order, to order the entry in the book of perpetual warning of ongoing exploratory proceedings and, in the case of a non-decision decision which has had irreversible legal effects, also to prohibit the disposal or debit of immovable property. Such a request shall be submitted by the committee. Where information is received on the proceedings pending before the administrative authority, the administrative court, the general court or the Supreme Court relating to the reprivatisation decision or the proceedings for payment of compensation or remuneration for the use of immovable property following a reprivatisation decision, the committee shall notify the authority or court of the initiation of an exploratory procedure. The proceedings before the authority or court shall be suspended. Without mentioning the subsequent powers of the committee, it can generally be concluded that, according to this proposal, the committee becomes a quasi-court, has a very extensive competence and, as the previous speaker said, combines practically all possible branches of power which are provided for by the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, while lacking the attributes of independence and impartiality during proceedings. It is a totally party-government initiative. It is worth noting that today some of the opposition tabled a draft resolution to set up a committee of inquiry, which will not, like this bill submitted, focus only on the situation of m.st. Warsaw, but it will deal with the situation of Poland as a whole, the pathology we have had since 27 years, concerning the bandit reprivatization and all abuses related to this practice. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The design, which is proposed in this project, is in our view incompatible with the constitution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-02-sejm-31-4.u442	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law on specific rules for the removal of legal effects of reprivatization decisions concerning Warsaw real estate issued in violation of law, print No. 1056. The purpose of the bill is to restore the protection of the social interest by removing the infringements that occurred during the adoption of the decision on the basis of the provisions of the Decree of 26 October 1945 on the ownership and use of land in the area of m.st. Warsaw, called the decree of Bierut. Reprivatisation is a process involving the return of nationalised private property. The problem of reprivatisation was addressed by the vast majority of post-communist countries, where, as a result of socio-economic transformation in the early 1990s, the process of returning the property previously acquired by the State was initiated. Today's widely understood reprivatisation is primarily about the problem of the settlement of claims to property by former owners. The specific nature of reprivatization in Warsaw is special in that it is a reversal of the effects of nationalization carried out on the basis of the decree of Bierut, which caused the acquisition of all the land within the borders of the then Warsaw by m.st. Warsaw. Bierut's decree is still a common law. In the hierarchy of law in Poland it is equal to the law, hence the repeal of its legislation requires the intervention of the legislator. The lack of initiative by the legislator over the past 27 years has led to what is now called wild reprivatization in Warsaw. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Through two terms the Civic Platform to the PSL community had time to regulate the conditions for reprivatization in Warsaw and prevent criminal, wild reprivatization by means of the law. They didn't do it. Everything points out that the President of Warsaw Hanna Gronkiewicz-Waltz premeditatedly took advantage of her position in the party and, acting to the public detriment, took care of the interests of her family and the interests of smart guys who developed sophisticated methods of taking over their undue property. It is high time to put an end to this and to repair the damage done. The Free and Solidarni Circle supports the work of a law aimed at removing the legal consequences of criminal activity in the course of wild reprivatisation, the activity of forgery of documents, procedural infringements in the course of administrative proceedings, infringements related to the abuse of personal rights in civil trade, infringements of the social interest, which deprives residents of the rights of residents of the property concerned of the reprivatisation proceedings. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-02-sejm-31-4.u468	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have a few comments. At this point, there is no government position on the bill. The amount of free money was widely discussed in previous meetings. We would like to stress that the argument that no one will benefit from the amount of free tax proposed in the already passed, passed and published act on personal income tax is incorrect. According to our best calculations, 3 million Poles will benefit from this. We therefore believe that today there is no need to verify or increase this amount, especially since, in the current legal state, the amount fixed for the next year is fully implemented by the Constitutional Tribunal. The second element of the proposed law is, in our opinion, a change in the basic logic in which local government units have a share in the revenue of the budget or the income of the public finance sector from personal income tax. Valorisation mechanism effectively causes that the revenues of local government units are no longer linked to current or real PIT revenues, but to some historical value re-valorized annually. This from our point of view will have a very negative effect - the effect of Petrifying the income ratio of individual municipalities and local government units regardless of natural migratory movements. If people emigrate from one municipality to the larger centres, the smaller municipality will have historical income from PIT, and the municipality to which citizens will arrive will, so to speak, be underfunded, which is likely to force another valorisation mechanism, which undermines the sense of this proposal that I have in front of me. Another negative effect of your proposal is that local government units will in no way be motivated to increase their tax base, to increase their income base from their own participation in PIT. Why? That's because they're going to rely on the automation of the valuer mechanism, not the downloading of investments, the building, as I say, of the tax base in personal income tax. The proposed law also causes significant perturbation for the public finance sector through precisely this valorisation mechanism, which, over a period of several years, will make the transfer from the budget to local government units, that the budget will have no part in the income from PIT, that the entire income from PIT will be transferred with the title of revalorisation or valorisation of the share of local government units, which effectively deprives the budget of at least a zloty of PIT income, which in itself constitutes a destabilisation of public finances. For this reason, the Ministry of Finance must express its opposition and protest against such proposals. Thank you.

It is about abolishing pension privileges arising from the high salaries provided by the communist state to security officers, especially those who showed zeal and availability. It is also a privilege to establish pension and pension benefits on a much more favourable basis under the Supply Law as regards how the vast majority of citizens are determined on the basis of the Social Insurance Fund Pension Act, including those repressed by the security apparatus of the Polish People's Republic, who are often unemployed, and thus the chances of a fair retirement. As a basis for the pension or invalidity pension in the service supply system, the allowance due in the last occupied post, i.e. as a general rule the highest in the entire career, and the amount of the pension or invalidity pension may be up to 80% in the case of a pension or 90% in the case of a pension in the case of an invalidity pension of that salary. While such a way of determining pension and pension benefits of civil servants protecting the security of the state, citizens and public order in democratic Poland is justified, it is unacceptable to former officials serving for the benefit of the totalitarian state, i.e. in bodies which systematically violated human rights. The solutions currently proposed are not repressive, they do not assume responsibility for the criminal acts committed during the period of the Polish People's Republic, nor do they replace such responsibility, but merely take away wrongly granted privileges. There is no reason for former officials serving the totalitarian state to receive pensions in amounts grossly above the average amount of benefits paid under the general pension scheme. It should be stressed that in the Third Republic there have been attempts to regulate this matter effectively on the basis of the principle of justice. Bachelor of the Order of the White Eagle former Prime Minister Jan Olszewski, in the times of the Polish People's Republic defender in political processes, evaluates the changes proposed by us as needed and adds: It is a pity that they are so late. The responsibility is borne by the then President Lech Wałęsa, who strongly opposed the changes we proposed and blocked them. Therefore, these changes are almost a quarter of a century late. I told him, "Man, you were chasing my family, my mom had one son in prison, another one in the underground, and my sister was arrested. And you have the audacity to demand a great pension from Poland? There's no reason to defend the esbeks. If there is a way to reduce their pensions to PLN 2100 according to the Constitution, it will not be a starving pension. Yes, sir, ladies and gentlemen, yes, High Sejm. Indeed, I am presenting a way to make amends to justice. It should be noted that the solutions introduced on the basis of the so-called. the first dehubian act of 23 January 2009 did not prove to be fully effective as its objective was not achieved to the desired extent. I'll get back to my previous thesis. Following the entry into force of the 2009 Act, pension benefits for persons who served in the State Security Authorities were re-calculated and the amount of individual police pensions was reduced to varying degrees. For example, a pension after 25 years of service determined on the basis of Article. The 15th Supply Act accounted for 66% the fundamentals of the dimension. After the entry into force of the Act, the pension authority received information from the Institute of National Remembrance about the conduct of the service, which indicated that the officer served for 20 years in the State Security Authority. Once the pension is re-established on the basis of Article. 15b of the Supply Act, the pension was 27% the basis of the dimension, i.e. more than half less, but this is not the end, because many provisions of the Supply Act granted the possibility of increasing the reduced pension, e.g. as a result of invalidity arising as a result of service, working under special conditions or in a specific character, increasing the basis of assessment for each year of premium periods before service and after service until 1 January 1999 at a working time of not less than half the full time, and e.g. for periods of payment of pension and pension contributions after 31 December 1998 Moreover, an important factor affecting the lack of the intended effectiveness of the 2009 de-zubekisation law was that it was covered only by pensioners, while leaving it outside of its influence by pensioners and persons entitled to a survivor's pension, since it should be noted that many people who receive uniform pensions also have a fixed right to an invalidity pension. After a reduction in pensions, they have benefited from the possibility of collecting disability pensions in the same old-age pension or less. Thus, the debubecisation law had no effect on the renners who served in the state security bodies in 1944 and 1990. It is stressed that, on the basis of the information provided by the IPN on the conduct of the service in the State security authorities, the procedure for re-calculating the service was also subject to survivor's pensions collected after the deceased pensioners who served in the security bodies of the communist state, but a relatively small number of these benefits were reduced. The position of the courts has been established that survivor's pensions should not be converted and the pension authority has therefore waived the re-fixing of family pensions on the basis of information received from the IPN on the conduct of the service in the State security authorities. In the assessment of the courts - this is extremely important - if the aim of the legislator was to apply the revised rules for calculating pensions to other benefits, e.g. Family pensions, that would be reflected in the legal act. Therefore, in view of the fact that the provision of pension supplies to all officers who have served as totalitarian service to the state, both those receiving pensions and invalidity pensions, as well as the survivor's pensions after those officers, should be reduced in real terms, new legal arrangements should be introduced. I am putting forward such legal solutions to the High House. High Seym! The proposed law provides for the reduction of pensions and invalidity pensions for all officers who served the totalitarian state and family pensions collected after such officers, in accordance with the following assumptions. Retirement. In the case of a person serving the totalitarian service of the State, the amount of the pension may not be higher than the monthly amount of the average pension paid by the Social Insurance Institution, which will be announced by the President of the Social Insurance Institution in the Official Journal of the Republic of Poland. According to ZUS data in June 2016, this amount was PLN 2053,50, so the pension will not be higher than this amount. This maximum pension will be determined after all possible increases have been made, in particular after counting the years of work before and after service. The maximum amount of the police pension adopted above, at the level of the average pension paid by the Social Security Office, leaves police pensioners covered by the proposed legislation in a relatively favourable situation, since, according to the information provided by the Social Security Office, 2 010 800 pensions are paid over the average amount, which is 39.6% all pensions paid by the Social Security Office, but 3 071 500 pensions are paid below the average amount, accounting for as much as 60.4% all pensions paid by the Social Security Office. Despite pension cuts, people covered by the new law will continue to receive higher benefits than 60% Polish pensioners. Disabled pension. In the case of a person serving a totalitarian service, the amount of the invalidity pension may not be higher than the monthly amount of the average invalidity pension paid by the Social Security Office, which will be published by the President of the Social Security Office in the Official Journal, as was the case in the case of a pension. According to ZUS data in June 2016, this amount was PLN 1543.05. The maximum amount of invalidity pension accepted above, at the level of the average disability pension paid by the Social Security Office, leaves the police disability pensioners covered by the proposed rules in a relatively favourable situation. Again, the information provided by the Social Security Office shows that 308 200 incapacity pensions are paid above the average amount, which represents 32,4% all invalidity pensions and 642 600 invalidity pensions are paid below the average amount, accounting for up to 67.6% all invalidity pensions paid by the Social Security Office. And in this case, people covered by the new regulation will continue to receive higher benefits than 2/3 of Polish invalids despite reductions in the amount of their pensions. In the context of the proposed amendment to the Supply Law, it should be noted that the 2009 Act has been the subject of a ruling by the Constitutional Tribunal. The Constitutional Tribunal stated that the legislator, acting as a law in 2009, did not infringe the substance of the right to social security. Although the reduction in pension benefits of the security authorities officers of the PRL is considered to be significant, it falls within the scope of the freedom of the legislator designated by the Constitution. A breach of the social security right would have occurred if the legislator had taken away their pension rights or reduced them to below the poverty line. We don't do that. That's not what this project assumes. By reducing pension benefits for selected groups, the legislator has not only ensured that pension benefits may not be lower than the lowest pension in the general social security system, but also ensured that the average pension rate under the new rules remains significantly higher than the average pension paid under the general insurance system. The maximum amounts of benefits introduced by the proposed provisions were set at the level of the average equivalent of those benefits paid by the Social Security Office. It should be stressed that a significant majority of pensions from the general social security system are paid below average benefits. The proposed rules therefore do not impose an excessive burden on the police pensioners and the persons entitled to receive police family pensions. In addition, it should be pointed out here that inequalities between the general social security system and the uniform system need to be bridged to the extent that they relate to former officials of the State security apparatus in the age of demographic crisis threatening the fundamentals of the existing universal social security system. In order to justify the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, it is necessary to state that the provisions of the 2009 Act are neither intended to deprive or humiliate the social minimum of security officers. The aim is to reduce pension benefits that come from a privileged pension supply system. That's what it is. It's the taking away of undue privileges. The Court found that, after the entry into force of the provisions of the 2009 Act, the average reduced pension of an officer of the security authorities was still higher by 58% than the average pension in the general pension scheme and almost four times the lowest guaranteed pension from the Social Insurance Fund. The Court also noted that the legislator was authorised, despite the expiry of the term of office of the PRL, for more than 19 years from the change in the system to the introduction of regulations reducing pension benefits for the period of service in the security bodies of the Polish People's Republic. The Court also found that the protection of acquired rights does not imply the integrity of those rights and does not preclude the regulation of less favourable ones. The Court explained that service in the institutions and bodies of a State which systematically violated natural human rights and the rule of law cannot justify claims in a democratic legal state to maintain the privileges acquired before the fall of dictatorial regimes. In this judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal noted that the aim of the legislature, which was to reduce pensions to the average level in the general social security system for the security authorities of the People's Republic of Poland. The resulting effect indicates that the average pension of an officer of the security authorities of the People's Republic of Poland in January 2010 was less than PLN 500, still exceeding the average pension paid in the general social security system. Such a reduction in pension benefits limits, and does not structurally abolish excessive, unfairly acquired high benefits of PRL security officers. Such a restriction also indicates that the aim of the legislator was not revenge on officers of the security authorities of the People's Poland. With regard to the abovementioned statement of the Constitutional Court, it should be reiterated that the aim set by the legislator, i.e. the reduction in pensions and pensions, has not been achieved. This objective was not achieved under the 2009 Act. Therefore, repairing this state of affairs constitutes the basic ratio of legis of the proposed law. That's it, ladies and gentlemen. This is the repair of what was not carried out in 2009. In addition to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, the broad case law of the general courts on the grounds of appeal against decisions determining the new amounts of pension benefits as a result of the 2009 Act is required. In particular, there is a significant resolution of the Supreme Court of 3 March 2011. In the view of the Supreme Court, the 2009 Act does not constitute any breach of the general principle of equality or proportionality of benefits derived from the provision of pension services by persons serving in totalitarian security bodies of a State who have used unlawful, unworthy or wicked acts or methods of political violence against other citizens, compared to the standards of benefits derived from the universal social security system. In the view of the Supreme Court, it is worth to draw attention to the still less favourable regulation of pension rights of victims of the political repression system applied by the security authorities of the totalitarian state. So once again, I would like to stress that we are repairing what was corrupted in the 2009 Act. I don't judge intent, but I haven't reached the goal it was set. The Supreme Court has explicitly assessed, assessed and disqualified the use of any arguments aimed at maintaining the privileges acquired in connection with participation in the system of enslavement and the application of political repression by the security services of the totalitarian state. Such privileges are not protected in the democratic order of national, Community and international law, because those who fight against natural human and national rights and fundamental civil liberties do not have the right, subjective or moral title, to demand the maintenance of supply privileges for periods of enslavement of independence, freedom and democracy of the Polish people. The European Court of Human Rights recognised that the limitation of pension rights for former security officers was within the limits of the state's margin of assessment and aimed at removing inequalities between the uniform system and the general system, which were generally considered unfair and requiring a state response. The system applicable to former officials was considered by the European Court to be double uneven, since on the one hand it set up a system of calculations more favourable to beneficiaries than a system of universal social security, and on the other hand, the calculations were carried out in proportion to the salaries which, in a group of former security officers, were statistically higher than those of non-professionals in support of the communist regime. In addition, the above mentioned paragraph is replaced by the following: The ruling of the European Court indicates that, for various reasons, political or factual, it is sometimes impossible to adopt such rules in post-communist countries earlier. Therefore, the passage of time since the overthrow of the regime should not be a decisive factor in the inadequacy of such correction of the pension system. I refer here to the words of Prime Minister Jan Olszewski. It wasn't possible then, it's possible today, it's possible today. The special issue of a privileged position in terms of pension rights enjoyed by people belonging to the communist elite or political police in post-communist European countries has already been examined by the Court on several occasions. In these cases, the European Court considered that the objective of eliminating or reducing unjustified or excessive social security benefits implemented by national legislators is appropriate, in particular the national legislature has the right to remove the political financial privileges granted to former officials by totalitarian regimes. This can be done provided that the steps taken are not disproportionate. Under the customary law of the European Court of Human Rights, there is no doubt that those enjoying such privileges have no justification to expect them to maintain their privileged position in this respect after the change to a democratic system. The European Court found that the Polish Parliament, having regard to the morally reprehensible role played by the Security Service, the political police in strengthening the communist system and repression of organisations and people defending democracy, and the fact that people employed in this service were granted various, including economic, privileges, to the effect that their continued use of favourable pension rights could not be reconciled with the principle of social justice. In conclusion, it should be stressed that both the Constitutional Tribunal and the European Court of Human Rights have recognised that the solutions introduced by the 2009 Act are in line with the Constitution, as well as the Convention for the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms respectively. The solutions are now being proposed further than those introduced in 2009, because that was the intention, but they do not affect the substance of the right to social security, while they are necessary to eliminate the further advantages of former civil servants serving the totalitarian state and those who are subsequently entitled to family pensions in the area of pensions. The transitional provisions proposed in the proposed law require the pension authority to initiate an ex officio procedure to re-establish the amount of benefits to former officials who served as totalitarian officials and other widows. Retirement and pension benefits will be paid from 1 October 2017. The indicated date of first payment is due to the fact that all reduced benefits should be paid within a single period, and given the deadline available to the Institute of National Remembrance for issuing the relevant information, and the minimum time required for staff of the unit serving the pension authority to modify the IT system and to issue a decision, it is not possible to specify a shorter period. Decisions re-setting the amount of the pension will be issued at different dates, depending on the moment of impact of the relevant information from the National Remembrance Institute, while all benefits should be paid for the first time at new levels within the same period. The time needed to carry out the design and implementation work and to carry out comprehensive tests of the modifications made to the IT system currently in operation to bring it into line with the new legislation is 7 months. The transitional provisions also provide that appeal to the court against the decision of the pension authority shall not suspend the implementation of that decision. This provision is necessary for the effective and as soon as possible reduction of pension and pension benefits of former officials serving the totalitarian state and widows and widows after them, as judicial proceedings in this area may take years. This is indicated by previous experiences. In view of the fact that so far the monthly amount paid by the Social Insurance Institution for the average pension, invalidity pensions and survivor's pension have not been declared, it was proposed that the President of the Social Insurance Institution should announce within 1 month of the date of entry into force of the Act, in the form of a communication in the Official Journal of the Republic of Poland, the Monitor of Poland, calculated for the last month of the quarter preceding the date of the publication of the Act, in the form of a communication in the Official Journal of the Republic of Poland. the amounts that will form the basis for fixing the maximum amounts of new pensions. The maximum amounts indicated above will be valued, which is guaranteed by the provisions introduced in the Supply Act. We propose that the proposed law should enter into force on 1 January 2017, with the exception of the provisions on the repeal of the Article. Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 1b and Article 1. 15b which should enter into force on 1 October 2017. Such a solution is necessary due to the length of the period from the date of entry into force of the Act to the date of the first payment of benefits in new amounts. High Seym! ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-02-sejm-31-4.u538	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Next Sunday, December 4th, we will celebrate an important anniversary. 90 years ago, on the initiative of Roman Dmowski, the Great Poland Camp was established. The organization was a cross-party structure, not involved in current political disputes, but forming the future elite of the Polish national movement, and was also preparing to take over power in our country in the future. The OWP had disciplined, hierarchical structures. She had the greatest influence in Wielkopolska, Pomerania, Silesia, Warsaw and all academic centres. The most popular was among young people, students, intellectuals and among the broadly understood middle class. However, young workers and peasants also joined her ranks. During the founding convention, Roman Dmowski's ideal father said: I have a deep faith that we will develop a great work quickly, which will bring great fruitage, that we will not be able to regret this work and will not be afraid of any obstacles that can stand in our way. In the name of God, go ahead! The main governing body of the camp was the Great Council, which included Roman Dmowski as a great devout and inter alia. Tadeusz Bielecki and Roman Fisherman. The OWP programme is presented in the brochures prepared by Roman Dmowski (the issue of the government, the Church, the nation and the state), Roman Fisherman (the politics and farm), Jerzy Zdziechowski (the financial policy), Bogdan Wasiutyński (the rule of law) and Zygmunt Berezowski (the foreign policy). The problem of the German minority and in general the issue of the German threat was recognised - a far-reaching goal was formulated to recover the native Slavic lands of the western part of the region, including Upper and Lower Silesia, Western Wielkopolska and Pomerania, as well as Warmia and Mazury. There were clear wings in the sphere of the economy. They were creatively different. On the one hand, there were representatives of free-market views, such as Rybarski or Zdziechowski, on the other hand, we observed the demands of the young people who promoted social reforms in the corporate spirit. The camp of Great Poland gradually grew into the strongest political movement in Poland, with up to 300 thousand. members and sympathizers. The Autonomous Sanitation Authority, sensing a real threat to itself, began repression against the members of the OWP. As early as May 1927, 15 leading activists were arrested. The increase in repression occurred in the early 1930s. From June to October 1932 150 trials of the members of the Great Poland Camp took place in Wielkopolska alone. The authorities have gradually begun to solve the regional camps. Finally, on 28 March 1933 the camp of Great Poland was dissolved throughout the country. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-02-sejm-31-4.u539	Thank you, sir. That concludes our parliamentary statements.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-02-sejm-31-4.u540	Declaration by the Members on the 100th anniversary of Wenceslas Grabowski's birth. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! December 10th this year It's the 100th birthday of Wenceslas Grabowski's pseudonym. It's a trash can. On this occasion, it is worth to approximate the life story of this commander of the last independence division operating until 5 July 1953 in northern Mazowsze. Wacław Grabowski was born in Krępa. His parents were Janina from the house of Lushczyk and Stanisław Grabowski. He had five siblings - two brothers and three sisters. In 1926. Grabowski became a member of ZHP in Mława, which he belonged to for 12 years. On 12 July 1936, he completed his second degree military adoption. He passed high school in Mława a few months before the war. In 1939, he participated in the defense war as a volunteer. During the German occupation he was a soldier of the AK Armed Fighting Union in the Mława region. In 1943, he began serving in guerrilla troops. He served in Kedywa, [[Head of Division of the National Army Headquarters]] as well as in the lieutenant's department. Stefan Rudziński's pseudo. Victor. After the entry of the Red Army, he acted in the structure of the Resistance of the Home Army under the battalion of Znicz, which was commanded by Capt. Paweł Nowakowski pseudod. Bald. He participated in an effective action of 6 June 1945 in Mława, during which about 30 prisoners were released. When the branch was dissolved in 1945, Grabowski and Anton Wójcicki entered the U.S. occupation zone in Germany. For a time he served in Polish Wartery Companies. He returned to his homeland after a very short emigration. He wanted to help the anti-communist defense. At that time he created a branch which belonged to the XVIth District of the National Military Union and operated in the districts of Mława, Przasnysz, Działdowo and Ciechanów. The most famous feat of the branch was to break through the UB ambush and shoot two MBP officers (Ministry of Public Security) and one CWB soldier (Inner Security Corps). After the liquidation of Puszczyk's branch, he made contact with Mieczysław Roj Dziemiewicz. From the autumn of 1952 to the summer of 1953, Puszczyka's branch stayed in colonial farms in Niedzięki. In July, a UB agent named Wacław Głuszek, who was paid 5,000 as a reward, released the location of the branch. zł. On 5 July 1953, Puszczyk's branch was surrounded by a 130-person operating group consisting of UB and MO officers. The Puszczyk troops didn't put up any weapons, still resisting the attacks. Wacław Grabowski's last bullet was meant for himself. Apartment Niedziek, Marianna Jeziorski pseudod. Grześ, for sheltering soldiers from the Lt. The owl was arrested and sentenced to a long-term prison sentence. She died on July 18, 1955 in the prison of Warsaw-Mokotów. A symbolic cross was placed at the cemetery in Lipowiec Kościelny. At the site of the last battle of Puszczyk's unit, in Niedzki, the families of the victims, local authorities and residents founded a plaque commemorating the solid soldiers. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-02-sejm-31-4.u541	Declaration on the control of hospital meals by the Trade Inspection and the State Sanitary Inspection. In the first half of this year, the health centre inspected food in 284 hospitals, which themselves prepare meals for patients. At the same time, the Trade Inspection commissioned by the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection audited 99 catering companies preparing ready meals and delivering them to 234 hospitals. The irregularities most frequently observed in the checks concerned mainly non-compliance with hygiene and sanitation requirements and non-compliance with the nutritional requirements of patients. In turn, the Commercial Inspection found irregularities in 61 catering facilities preparing ready meals and delivering them to hospitals - 62% examined - on the previously mentioned 99 tested. In most cases, the expiry dates of the products for consumption were also not respected. Patients were given meals that had an undersized portion relative to the declaration set out in the menu. The reservations concerned 13 catering companies and 15 parties on 155 tested in this respect. At times, as many as 62% were underweight. As an example, the portions of pork chops were lighter by between 5 g and 50 g, while, according to the menu, the weight of one portion should be 80 g. According to a communication on the website of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection - you can find this information - meals were also checked in the laboratories of the Office by verifying their actual composition. The objections raised concerned six entrepreneurs and seven parties per 75 examined. Here, too high gluten content in gluten-free sauce can be used as an example - 30 mg/kg gluten was found. This can be harmful for people who are allergic to this ingredient and patients with coeliac disease. According to the regulations, gluten-free product may contain up to 20 mg/kg gluten. In addition, it should be noted that, in several cases, contracts between hospitals and catering companies were missing, for example. detailed rules for the provision of meals or contractual penalties for improper performance of the contract. I therefore call on the High House to make changes, legislative processes that will contribute to increasing supervision of companies supplying food to hospitals. Thank you very much.
